# AL–TN–KY lead sheet

**November 21, 2026 · Finder to Dash and the Stringer · Alabama county research in progress**

This sheet holds **487 distinct additions and 37 HAVE cross-references** across 524 rows. The latest dated batch below closes the Barbour and Bibb source inventories and opens Blount County. Access assessments reflect the cited public evidence.
 
## Intake and held ground

- The [live homepage](https://onoldground.com/) and [full catalog](https://onoldground.com/catalog) returned **647 places across 23 states** at the Barbour intake, including 48 in Mississippi. The preceding pass read 640. Alabama, Tennessee and Kentucky each had zero entries. Collision checks used place names, aliases, towns, the [national index](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1qczgCCPGDz7KZ7zg2ztdgXPpxM98MFgd/view), the regional and haunted sweeps, and the shared state sheets.
- The [regional first sweep](https://drive.google.com/open?id=10btK8-_-sHk2WnhHNXyhgBQABW60Ij5v) holds 151 rows, including 48 in Alabama. The [haunted sweep](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1fk66ImafhQVjPPwQruHJ4nbFa9hahtHc) holds 18 Alabama rows. Six overlap with the regional sweep, giving 60 distinct Alabama places across those two lists. Several also appear in the state sheets.
- The [Alabama operations sheet](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1ZFvDIt3lsn0cu9PFBZF5bAEEXoobUDjU) already held five of the first 39 rows here. The five are marked **HAVE** below. The revised first-pass count is 34 additions. The [revised Alabama](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1SBUVhvbdTTzn-O0fflv3JkOvrDGaO_ST), [first Tennessee](https://drive.google.com/open?id=12ec2J08qm9oHqFc-VomBjZBYcPKuf06_), [revised Tennessee](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1pN8J69UsOrhucbBEqP7wnIK-uUvpugoP) and [Kentucky](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1U49JcP4rhkA89RJV5G7SPrwqavJzbhP4) sheets were also checked before handoff.
- All 18 [LA–MS supplement readings](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1-75le2JvN4H1vNnFbA3WD_P-8Hb8Hx_v) have nonempty drafts in their state lanes, nine in [Louisiana](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/13_jDLLyVLCdBQFNObyxF9_MTFbvSvydk) and nine in [Mississippi](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1BDzwj24lPP9jX9c13MdgIYddVyMUrJcP).

- **Barbour intake, November 21.** The Stringer’s [latest regional state block](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1gxEkQwVW4l-kgHpZ2-4FNvdoqFY3Ic34) keeps Mississippi active. All 18 named institutional readings were matched again to nonempty files in their actual Louisiana and Mississippi dossier folders, nine each. No supplement handoff remains. The regional sheet already holds Henry D. Clayton House and Shorter Mansion with its district. Alabama Operations already holds Fendall Hall. Federal district inventories were checked by building name and address before treating a building as distinct.
- **Stringer at handoff.** [Mississippi OP15](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1ASq5aqMAUSxhk2H0iLlzwL4YOMbeZj5-) closed four north Mississippi subjects during this pass. Mississippi remains the Stringer’s active state.
- **Adopted owner rulings.** The road-trip core is the 48 contiguous states and Alaska via the Alaska Highway. Hawaii is a fly-in variant outside the march, sweep lists and totals. Free records carry the claims. A paywalled source can supply a lead. An unavailable free record stays a gap. Historical photographs require personal inspection, verified public-domain status and printed credit. Dash applies these rules on desk return of earlier filings as well as on new work. [Scope](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1-S9OYkWL8uOsF3rpVMQRvzEmt-hnpMpN) · [Source standard](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1oAkq1GG6owK_gR4IXH8Hycmi1tztv9Rd)

## Reading the tiers

Tier 1 has substantial published visitation. Tier 2 has a national landmark or statewide destination designation. Tier 3 has a regional or local signal, such as an official visitor program, public marker or historic-register recognition. Tier 4 has **NO SIGNAL** from the records checked. Designation is a recognition signal. Attendance is given only when a source reports it. A designation or attendance figure for a replacement venue is attributed to that venue. **NONE** means no lawful ordinary public anchor was verified. It includes lost sites and occupied private or institutional property. **Seasonal** includes recurring event-only openings.

## Coverage and resumption point at the preceding handoff

- The [federal roster of November 20](https://ahc.alabama.gov/nationalregisterPDFs/2026/National_Register_Properties_in_Alabama%2011_20_26.pdf) lists 22 distinct properties across Coffee (5), Dale (4) and Houston (13). All 22 are accounted for. Dothan Main Street’s boundary update stays with its original district.
- All 42 entries in the [printed state roster](https://ahc.alabama.gov/AlabamaRegisterPDFs/Alabama_Register_of_Landmarks_%26_Heritage_Property_Listing_as_of_10_22_2026.pdf) for Coffee (14), Dale (11) and Houston (17) are accounted for. Thirteen were already identified. Of the remaining 29, 28 yielded new places and one yielded a HAVE cross-reference for the Little Red Schoolhouse at the Depot Museum. Brown’s Crossroad Schoolhouse is already represented within Landmark Park. The federal and state rosters overlap.
- Three further records in the [commission’s mapped inventory](https://services2.arcgis.com/XBn0Kai3hQ20FeCo/arcgis/rest/services/Alabama_Register_4_view/FeatureServer) yielded new leads. They are Greater Old Salem Baptist Church in Dale County, and New Maranda Baptist Church and Columbia Presbyterian Church in Houston County. New Maranda appears under Henry County in the printed roster.
- **Barbour County.** All 19 listings in the federal roster and 16 in the state roster are accounted for. Woodlane appears in both, leaving 34 distinct printed-register subjects. One further site, Historic Saint Luke AME Church, came from the state map. Six off-register leads address incarceration, voting rights, funerary lore, Black burial, railroad reuse and federal conservation. The combined check produced 41 rows, of which 23 add new subjects and 18 point to held ground. Four rows cross-reference the held Shorter Mansion, its district, Henry D. Clayton House and Fendall Hall. Fourteen more address buildings within the held district. No additional lead is counted for Woodlane’s second registration.
- **Next sources.** Continue Barbour County beyond the two registers. Start with county hospital and poor-relief records, historic airfields and other military installations, Muscogee-authored site histories, and county cemetery and marker inventories beyond the two burial places checked here. Then advance the county register sweep into Bibb County, beginning with Blocton Italian Catholic Cemetery and Brierfield Furnace. Pike and Mobile are also open. Tennessee and Kentucky have separate holdings to check before any additions.

**Earlier state block · Barbour register pass**

- **Leads filed** — 23 new Barbour County leads and 18 HAVE cross-references added to the canonical sheet. The preceding 65 new leads and six HAVE rows remain. The 18 institutional supplement readings are all HAVE.
- **Coverage math** — 88 distinct additions plus 24 HAVE equals 112 rows. New this pass: 2 verified open, 2 exterior only and 19 NONE. Cumulative distinct additions: 15 verified open, 5 seasonal, 15 exterior only, 53 NONE. The 49-state road-trip core excludes Hawaii. Barbour printed rosters: 19/19 federal and 16/16 state.
- **Next territory** — Continue the Barbour County sources above, then the Bibb County register sweep within Alabama.
- **Blockers** — The present location of Eufaula’s relocated jail and a lawful standing place at the 1874 polling sites remain unverified. Keep those readings unrouted. The state register and federal district survey disagree on the address of the Joyce–Copeland–Reeves House.



## November 21, 2026 · Barbour completion, Bibb County and opening of Blount County


The live [homepage](https://onoldground.com/) and [catalog](https://onoldground.com/catalog) showed **675 places in 23 states** at **1:57 a.m. Pacific**. The rebuilt catalog groups entries before showing individual titles. I read both stop groups to the last page and matched all **675 unique entry links** to the live sitemap at **2:05:34 a.m. Pacific**. The final catalog and complete slug check at **2:15:56 a.m. Pacific** found the same 675. Alabama had zero live entries. The earlier figure of 647 belongs to the preceding batch. The desk checks again at dossier intake.

The [national index](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1qczgCCPGDz7KZ7zg2ztdgXPpxM98MFgd), [regional sweep](https://drive.google.com/open?id=10btK8-_-sHk2WnhHNXyhgBQABW60Ij5v), [haunted sweep](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1fk66ImafhQVjPPwQruHJ4nbFa9hahtHc), [Alabama operations sheet](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1ZFvDIt3lsn0cu9PFBZF5bAEEXoobUDjU), [its revision](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1SBUVhvbdTTzn-O0fflv3JkOvrDGaO_ST), earlier Finder rows and Tennessee/Kentucky sheets were checked by place and alias. The eighteen institutional readings again matched eighteen nonempty files, nine in each actual state dossier folder. [MS OP18](https://drive.google.com/open?id=1rnSBFlkc6-rI0Bt_kZgPBXe9kisvyPnh) keeps the Stringer in Mississippi.

### How the additional cemetery leads were counted


The commission’s October cemetery roster and its mapped records supply names, filing histories and, for some cemeteries, an explicit public-access classification. An exterior anchor based on those records is limited to maintained grounds marked public. Other burial places retain NONE unless a separate operator establishes access. All are reflection ground. Preservation flags reflect recorded conditions and direct the desk to check current status. No photography or contribution prompt is attached.

The University of Alabama supplies a county map key and a written inventory, including aliases, graves with no mapped position and same-name cemeteries in different places. Each numbered record was compared with the commission’s cemetery layers, the county’s existing ground and all names under that number. A survey index number identifies a document record. Named but unplotted grounds with identifiable historical burials can file as readings. Unnamed records and unresolved aliases stay outside the lead count. No visitation count is inferred from a survey listing.

### Barbour County · remaining classes


Salter Hospital and county poor relief have separate records. The Census identifies the Clayton almshouse, while the hospital operator dates the original institution and its move. Old Creek Town Park supplies an independently verified public place for the Creek removal commemoration. The Muscogee Nation’s own resource guide supplies the tribal-town connection. Keep the present jail location and the 1874 polling sites unrouted. The existing Joyce–Copeland–Reeves row retains **420 West Broad** and **420 West Barbour**.

#### County inventory reconciliation


**Cemetery register and atlas.** The October state cemetery register supplies 38 named rows. The university’s map key has 202 numbered locations. Of those, 130 are new numbered leads, 39 refer to ground already represented, and 33 are unnamed or have an unresolved identity. The written atlas adds 62 unplotted source rows: 16 became new historical readings, 14 have a possible same-name record, and 32 remain identity holds. Names below are research references only. [AHC cemetery register, October 2026](https://ahc.alabama.gov/cemeteryprogramPDFs/2026/Alabama_Historic_Cemetery_Register_10.2026.pdf) · [University of Alabama cemetery inventory, Barbour](https://alabamamaps.ua.edu/cemetery/text/Barbour.pdf) · [University of Alabama, county cemetery atlas map](https://alabamamaps.ua.edu/cemetery/maps/Barbour.pdf)

**Matched map records.** 2 Ramah Baptist Church, 20 Shady Grove Missionary Baptist Church Cemetery, 21 Evergreen Baptist Church Cemetery, 29 William T. Mullen whiskey-bottle gravestone / Clayton Cemetery, 30 Lightner Family Cemetery, 33 Old Mt. Nebo Cemetery (Flourney Cemetery), 37 Pioneer Black Cemetery, 38 Providence Methodist Church and Schoolhouse, 40 Beauchamp Family Cemetery, 50 Hunter-Merrill Cemetery, 51 Shorter Cemetery, 52 Old Fairview Cemetery, 54 McGee Cemetery, 58 Mt. Zion Baptist Church Cemetery, 59 Ephesus Cemetery, 60 Dykes Baptist Church Cemetery, 61 Mt. Nebo Missionary Baptist Church Cemetery, 62 Pea Creek Baptist Church Cemetery, 63 Grubbs Cemetery, 65 Saint Luke AME Church Cemetery, 71 Warr Hill Graveyard, 84 Walker Family Cemetery, 95 White Oak Chapel / Whiteoak United Methodist Church, 100 Big Creek Christian Baptist Fellowship Cemetery, 101 Faulk Church Cemetery, 102 Sutton Cemetery, 103 Dansby Family Cemetery, 110 Elam Baptist Church Cemetery, 111 Pea River Presbyterian Church Cemetery, 118 Helms Family Cemetery, 137 Bethsaida Cemetery, 162 Dawkins Family Cemetery, 166 Thornton Cemetery, 167 Louisville Methodist Church Cemetery, 168 Bennett-Lee Cemetery, 178 King Family Cemetery, 183 John’s Cemetery, 195 Craig Family Cemetery, 199 Scroggins Cemetery.

**Numbered identity holds.** 14 (possible Mt. Zion Baptist Church Cemetery — Mount Zion), 25 (unnamed), 26 (possible Pleasant Grove Baptist Church Cemetery — Pleasant Grove), 36 (unnamed), 43 (unnamed), 48 (possible Pleasant Grove Baptist Church Cemetery — Pleasant Grove), 56 (possible church or cemetery alias — Antioch), 66 (possible Mt. Zion Baptist Church Cemetery — Mount Zion), 70 (possible Mt. Zion Baptist Church Cemetery — Mount Zion), 74 (possible church or cemetery alias — Antioch), 86 (unnamed), 93 (unnamed), 98 (unnamed), 113 (possible church or cemetery alias — Antioch), 131 (unnamed), 140 (possible Elam Baptist Cemetery — Elam), 144 (unnamed), 148 (unnamed), 149 (possible church or cemetery alias — Bethel), 151 (unnamed), 157 (unnamed), 160 (unnamed), 163 (unnamed), 164 (unnamed), 165 (unnamed), 173 (unnamed), 174 (unnamed), 187 (possible church or cemetery alias — Bethel), 189 (possible Pea Creek Baptist Church Cemetery — Pea Creek), 190 (possible church or cemetery alias — Springhill), 196 (unnamed), 198 (possible Louisville Methodist Church Cemetery — Louisville), 201 (possible church or cemetery alias — Saint Luke).

**Unplotted cross-checks.** Anderson (compare 120), Batesville (compare 9), Creel (compare 127), Espy (compare 192), Faulk (compare 101), Johnson (compare 28), Jones (compare 13), Lee (compare Lee Family Cemetery), Lee (compare Lee Family Cemetery), Lee (compare Lee Family Cemetery), Lightner (compare 30), Salem (compare 64), St. Louis (compare 109), Unknown (compare 163).

**Additional unplotted identity holds.** Adams-Harrison, Baker, Bush, Chankey Penn, Charity Club, Davis, Davis, Emerson-Myers, Engram (assumed), Griffin, Gurley (assumed), Hagler, Holland, Hutto, Knight, Landmark, McCormick-Faulk, McGilvray, Millers, Phillips, Pomeroy, Sanders, Sheppard, St. Emmanuel, Streater, Thomas, Unnamed, Walter Mitchell, Warren (assumed), Warren (assumed), Wilson, Holland-Clark. Names repeated by the source are retained.

**State cemetery map spellings under review.** Blair / Bishop–Blair, Shorte / Shorter, Adam / Adams, Saint Peter / Saint Peters. A variant alone adds no new subject.

**Markers.** The Chattahoochee Commission texts for Grace Episcopal, Miller–Martin, the Octagon House, Spring Hill Methodist, Fairview and the 1874 violence refer back to earlier rows. The Old Creek Town, Little Scotland and Fort Browder texts are on the corresponding new rows. The state marker layer also points back to Fendall Hall, Old Fairview, Ramah, Pea River, McGee–Reeder, Historic St. Luke, Jones Chapel and King Family Cemetery. The Alabama Historical Association’s Eufaula text supplements the tribal-town trail. [Historic Chattahoochee Commission, sponsors and marker texts](https://histchattcom.wixsite.com/histchattcom/barbour-county) · [Alabama Historical Association, Barbour marker text](https://www.alabamahistory.net/copy-of-baldwin)

**Other military and institutional checks.** Civil aviation records verify Weedon Field. A wartime training, radar or missile role was not established. The state prison records establish Easterling and Ventress. No independent county sanatorium or asylum with a named site cleared this pass. The existing wartime hospital at The Tavern stays a district cross-reference. No new haunted telling was assigned to any cemetery from its name or appearance.

### Bibb County · two registers and county sweep


The six federal rows come first in the requested order. The twelve printed state rows follow, then grounds identified by the cemetery, marker, hospital, corrections, engineering, disaster and public-land sources. Brierfield Furnace is the already-held Brierfield Ironworks Historical State Park. The superintendent’s house has its own state listing on the same public park grounds.

#### County inventory reconciliation


**Cemetery register and atlas.** The October state cemetery register supplies 15 named rows. The university’s map key has 147 numbered locations. Of those, 124 are new numbered leads, 15 refer to ground already represented, and 8 are unnamed or have an unresolved identity. The written atlas adds 60 unplotted source rows: 23 became new historical readings, 11 have a possible same-name record, and 26 remain identity holds. Names below are research references only. [AHC cemetery register, October 2026](https://ahc.alabama.gov/cemeteryprogramPDFs/2026/Alabama_Historic_Cemetery_Register_10.2026.pdf) · [University of Alabama cemetery inventory, Bibb](https://alabamamaps.ua.edu/cemetery/text/Bibb.pdf) · [University of Alabama, county cemetery atlas map](https://alabamamaps.ua.edu/cemetery/maps/Bibb.pdf)

**Matched map records.** 1 Bibbville Church Cemetery, 9 Mt. Carmel Cemetery, 39 George Stewart Cemetery (Ada Chapel), 46 Haysop Baptist Church, 52 Blocton Italian Catholic Cemetery, 53 Shiny Rock Cemetery, 65 Cahawba Valley Baptist Church Cemetery, 70 Brierfield Cemetery, 99 Centreville Cemetery, 102 Bethal Baptist Church Cemetery, 109 Sandy Chapel Methodist Church, 116 Antioch Church Cemetery, 131 Unionship Baptist Church Cemetery, 134 Green Pond Presbyterian Church and Cemetery, 136 Smith-Mahan Cemetery.

**Numbered identity holds.** 26 (unnamed), 54 (unnamed), 83 (unnamed), 100 (unnamed), 143 (unnamed), 145 (unnamed), 146 (unnamed), 147 (unnamed).

**Unplotted cross-checks.** Belchers (compare 29), Friendship (compare 82), Macedonia (compare 80), McCulley (compare 36), Mulberry (compare 85), Murphy (compare 47), Piper (compare Piper Cemetery), Randolph (compare 120), Reach (compare 12), Smith (compare 90), Thompson (compare 61).

**Additional unplotted identity holds.** Acton, Bermy, Casey, Copper, Daniel-Blake, Gray (assumed), Hooper, Hutchins, Jackson, Jones, Jones, Latham, Latham, New Haven, New Hebron, Pierson, Pleasant Grove, Pleasant Sabine, Reese, Steele, Sudsbury, Sudsbury, New Hope (old), Old New Hope, Shultz Creek, Leach. Names repeated by the source are retained.

**State cemetery map spellings under review.** Daniel / Daniel–Blake, McKensey / McKinney, Shiney Rock / Shiny Rock, Six Mile / Six Mile Presbyterian, Johnstown / Johntown, Pineland / Pineland Memorial Park, New Prospect / Old Union, Six Mile Presbyterian / Six Mile. A variant alone adds no new subject.

**Markers and boundaries.** The county’s Alabama Historical Association markers cover the already-held Brierfield Naval Furnaces and Haysop Baptist Church. The commission map’s three markers match Six Mile Academy, Centreville Cemetery and Mt. Sinai Cemetery. Centreville’s historic jail is already inside the district. The Lucile Mine archive records convict leasing. The weather radar at Centreville was a civilian installation. The family sweep produced no verified county Cold War missile base or separate state asylum. Namesakes in other places, including The Oaks at Tuskegee, were excluded from same-site collisions.
